Can this be computed by convolution with kernel? Let sequence a length n. For each k, mapping from a_j to indices i=k-j. So contribution to grad_i from a_j with weight w_{i+j}. Specifically grad_i = 2∑_{j=0}^{n-1} w_{i+j} a_j, where w_k defined for k=0..2n-2. This is cross-correlation with weight matrix depending on sum i+j. Can compute via flips? Another potential approach: Use DFT and solve for A coefficients to make convolution coefficients target. Since c = A*A. If we choose DFT values U_m for m=0..L-1 (with U_0=S) and require A = IFFT(U) nonnegative. The convolution coefficients c have DFT C_m = U_m^2. We want C_m such that inverse c flat and nonnegative. We can choose U_m with phases to make c flat. This is like phase retrieval. For real a, U_{L-m}=conj(U_m). C_{L-m}=conj(U_m^2)? If U_{-m}=conj(U_m), then C_{-m}=conj(U_m^2). So C is Hermitian. We want c_k real nonnegative. We can choose U_m = sqrt(V_m) where V_m is Hermitian positive? Then C_m=V_m, c=IFFT(V). If V_m are nonnegative? Then c? We can choose V_m to make c flat, but then a=IFFT(sqrt(V)) may not be nonnegative. But we can optimize phases of U to make a nonnegative and c flat. This is a convex problem? Suppose we choose target c coefficients (nonnegative sum1). Then DFT C. Need find U such that U^2=C and a=IFFT(U)≥0. Is C a square in Fourier? The condition for C to be square of some U up to phase: C_m must be Hermitian and have a phase progression? Not restrictive? Given C, solving U_m = ±√|C_m| e^{iφ/2}; phases must satisfy φ_{-m}=-φ_m. Many choices. a real. Could select phases to make a nonnegative. Maybe use iterative projection: start c_flat, compute C, choose phases to make U with inverse nonnegative (phase retrieval), update c. This resembles CRLB.

def convolve_square_fft(a, L):
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A*A, L)
    # Due to numerical noise, c has tiny negative; for max no issue.
    return c[:2*len(a)-1]
```

def fast_score(a, L=None):

Gradient direct:
```
def grad_top_k(a, c, K, weights=None):
    n=len(a)
    if weights is None:
       idx = np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:] if K<n else range
       w=1/K for each
    g=np.zeros(n)
    for k,wk in zip(idx, weights):
       lo = max(0, k-n+1); hi=min(n, k+1)
       if lo<hi:
          g[lo:hi] += 2.0*wk * a[k-lo : k-hi : -1]
    return g
```
Need `argpartition` if K > c length? K up to n maybe. Since c length 2n-1. Top K among all conv coefficients. If K large e.g. 256, direct cost 256*n=1.2M, okay. But `argpartition` O(m) each iteration; m~9611, fine. Could use `np.argwhere(c >= threshold)` but no.

Line search mirror:
```
def mirror_update(a, g, eta, floor=1e-30):
    alpha = -eta * (g - g.mean())
    alpha = np.clip(alpha, -50, 50)
    a2 = a * np.exp(alpha)
    s=a2.sum()
    if s==0: return a.copy()
    a2 /= s
    a2 = np.maximum(a2, floor)
    a2 /= a2.sum()
    return a2
```
But subtracting mean can reduce step; if gradient important globally, maybe no mean. With normalization, constant shift irrelevant. For projection via mirror, project to simplex by multiplicative up; constant shift not matter because exp(α+c) scaled. So yes subtract mean.

Choosing eta: We can base on norm of g:
```
gvar = g - g.mean()
stdg = sqrt(mean(gvar*gvar))
if stdg < 1e-15: no change
lms = [0.1,0.3,1,3,10,30,100,300,1000] # these are multipliers of stdg? alpha = -lm * gvar / stdg? exponent std = lm.
```
If use alpha = -lm * gvar / stdg, exponent std=lm. Try lm values from 0.05 to 50. This decouples scale. For n=4806, stdg maybe 1e-4, making standardized alpha. Use candidates `[0.02, 0.05, 0.1, 0.3, 1.0, 3.0, 10.0, 30.0]`. Exponent up to 30 huge. We clip alpha to [-30,30]. Good.

Pseudo `run_mirror`:
```
def run_mirror(a, n, deadline, rng, tag):
    a = normalize(a)
    best_a = a.copy(); best_score=fast_score(a)
    L = get_len(2n-1)
    K = 16; K_stage=...
    lm = 1.0
    it=0; stall=0; last_improve=...
    while time<deadline:
       c=conv(a)
       M=c.max(); cur=2nM
       if cur<best: update
       # weights top K softmax
       if c.size <= 8*? K=c.size else K current
       top_idx = argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]
       top_c = c[top_idx]
       tau = max(top_c[0] - np.min(top_c), M*1e-4, 1e-12)
       w = exp((top_c - top_c[0])/tau); w/=w.sum()
       g = grad_top_k(a,c,top_idx,w)
       gvar = g - g.mean()
       std = rms
       if std < 1e-15: break? random perturb
       candidates=[]
       for lm_try in [lm, lm*0.25, lm*4]:
          alpha = -lm_try * gvar / std
          alpha=clip(alpha, -20, 20)
          p2 = a * exp(alpha)
          p2 = np.maximum(p2, floor)
          p2=p2/p2.sum()
          candidates.append((p2,lm_try))
       # score candidates, pick best (maybe also current if all worse)
       for p2, lm_try: sc=fast_score(p2)
       choose best candidate if sc < cur + accept_tol
       if best candidate sc < cur*(1-1e-9): accept; if sc<best update; lm=lm_try; stall=0
       else:
          # small random perturbation sometimes
          if rng.random()<0.05: p2 = perturb from best_a...
          # reduce lm
          lm *= 0.5
          stall +=1
          if stall > 200: K*=2 capped; if K>1024: reset from best with noise; lm=1
       if it % 20==0 print
    return best_a,best_score
```
Need avoid fast_score for each candidate too many; each fast_score does FFT. 3 per iteration maybe okay. We can reduce: evaluate only candidate with lm and if improves, maybe check smaller/larger only around. But optimization quality maybe. Let's do 3.

But FISTA uses gradient at y, then a_new; set a_prev=a_old; a=a_new; y=a_new next. Implement carefully:
```
a_prev = a.copy(); y=a.copy(); t=1
loop:
  c=conv(y); cur=score(y)

Detailed code:
```
import numpy as np
import time

def next_pow2(x):
    if x <=1: return 1
    return 1 << (int(np.ceil(np.log2(x))))
```
But `np.log2` okay. Or while loop.

`normalize`:
```
def normalize_arr(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a[np.isnan(a)|np.isinf(a)] = 0.0
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-30:
       return np.full_like(a, 1.0/a.size) if a.size else np.ones(1)
    a /= s
    return a
```

`conv_square_fft`:
```
def conv_square_fft(a, L=None):
    n = len(a)
    out_len = 2*n - 1
    if L is None: L=next_pow2(out_len)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, L)
    # Remove tiny negative
    c = c[:out_len]
    # maybe set small negative to 0 for max? max negative not matter; but for weights, negative could cause exp large? Use np.maximum(c,0) maybe but if all negative due to roundoff? max remains.
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)  # is this safe? It changes sum but score negligible. For weights, okay.
    return c
```
Clipping negative to 0 may slightly alter max if max negative? Not. Could alter shape; but negative roundoff ~1e-20, fine.

`conv_cross_fft`:

def conv_cross_fft(a,b,L): out=...; return irfft(rfft(a,L)*rfft(b,L))[:out_len]
```

`fast_score`:
```
def fast_score(a, L=None):
    n=len(a)
    c = conv_square_fft(a,L)
    M = c.max()
    return float(2.0*n*M)
```
Since normalized. If not normalized, use sum formula. We'll normalize candidates.

`weights_from_c`:
```
def weights_from_c(c, q=None, rel_floor=1e-4):
    maxc = c.max()
    if maxc <= 0: return np.full_like(c, 1.0/c.size)
    if q is None: q=0.99
    # For small arrays, q can be fractional
    thresh = np.quantile(c, q)
    tau = max(maxc - thresh, maxc*rel_floor, 1e-300)
    z = (c - maxc)/tau
    z = np.clip(z, -50, 0)
    w = np.exp(z)
    s=w.sum()
    if s <=0: ... uniform
    return w/s
```
If q=1.0? np.quantile(c,1.0)=max; tau floor; weights focus on max. We'll vary q: 0.99, if stall use 0.999 or 0.95. But lower q produces larger tau (broader), higher q smaller (sharper). Wait q=0.99 gives 99th, so max-thresh is top1% gap. q=0.999 sharper. If q=0.9 broader. We can start q=0.995, if no improvement reduce tau? To focus, increase q to 0.999. But if too focused (single peak) and stuck, broaden to 0.95. We'll adapt: when stall, first if q <0.999, q +=0.004 (sharper); if q>0.999 and still, broaden q=0.9.
- However if q too sharp, gradient top single; if symmetric center gradient constant, no movement. Need include neighboring peaks; q=0.995 top 48 coefficients for n=4806. Good. If single, broaden.
- Maybe use `q = 1.0 - float(K)/len(c)`, K maybe 64. Use K-based: `q = min(0.999, 1 - K/max(len(c),1))`. We can adapt K. Simpler: `K_weights` start 64; q = 1 - K/(len(c)) roughly. `grad_weighted_fft`:
```
def grad_weighted_fft(a, w, L=None, factor=2.0):
    n=len(a)
    if L is None: L=next_pow2(3*n-2)
    r=a[::-1]
    Rp=np.fft.rfft(r, L)
    Wp=np.fft.rfft(w, L)  # w length 2n-1
    conv=np.fft.irfft(Rp*W, L)
    g = conv[n-1:2*n-1]
    return factor * g
```
Need ensure `w` zero-padded length L by rfft. If `w` length >L? L≥3n-2 >2n-1, okay.
Potential real-result of irfft of product of rfft? For real conv, yes because product Hermitian? r and w real, full product Hermitian; rfft product? Should yield real. Use `.real`? irfft returns real.

Test with small maybe. Good.

`mirror_step`:
```
def mirror_step(a, g, lm, floor=1e-18):
    g = g - g.mean()
    std = np.sqrt(np.mean(g*g))
    if std <= 1e-30:
        return a.copy()
    alpha = -lm * g / std
    alpha = np.clip(alpha, -30, 30)
    a2 = a * np.exp(alpha)
    a2 = np.where(a2 > floor, a2, floor)
    s = a2.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300:
       return a.copy()
    a2 /= s
    return a2
```
Note passing lm already standardized exponent. We recompute std inside; then `run` could compute std to decide. To avoid double, return std separate? Simpler.

But if we standardize inside, all lm meaning exponent std. Good. For adjusting, use same. We don't need external std.

def run_mirror(a, n, deadline, rng, tag):
    Lc=next_pow2(2*n-1)
    Lg=next_pow2(3*n-2)
    a=normalize_arr(a)
    best_a=a.copy(); best_score=fast_score(a,Lc)
    cur=a.copy(); cur_score=best_score
    lm=1.0
    q=0.995
    stall=0; it=0; last_print=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       c=conv_square_fft(cur,Lc)
       M=c.max(); cur_score=2*n*M
       if cur_score < best_score - 1e-12:
           best_score=cur_score; best_a=cur.copy(); last_print=time...
       # if all flat? max-min small
       if M - c.min() < M*1e-7:
           # perturb
       w=weights_from_c(c,q)
       g=grad_weighted_fft(cur,w,Lg,2.0)
       # candidate with current lm
       p_try=mirror_step(cur,g,lm)
       sc_try=fast_score(p_try,Lc)
       accept=True; chosen=p_try; chosen_lm=lm
       if sc_try >= cur_score:
           # try smaller
           p_half=mirror_step(cur,g,lm*0.5); sc_half=fast_score(p_half,Lc)
           if sc_half < cur_score:
               chosen=p_half; chosen_lm=lm*0.5
           else:
               # try larger? sometimes large overshoot may improve nonmonotone? Not if half worse; larger likely worse.
               # nonmonotone accept if stall>50 and prob
               accept_flag = (stall > 50 and rng.random() < 0.05 and sc_try < cur_score * (1 + 1e-4))
               if accept_flag:
                   chosen=p_try; chosen_lm=lm
               else:
                   # no accept
                   cur_score_trial=cur_score
                   no_improve
       if accepted:
           cur=chosen; cur_score=fast_score(cur)? We already have; if chosen half score known; store.
           lm = chosen_lm * (1.1 if score improved else 1.0)
           lm = min(max(lm,0.05),50.0)
           stall=0
       else:
           lm *=0.7
           stall +=1
           if stall > 100:
              # broaden/sharpen q
              if q < 0.999 and stall % 50 ==0: q += 0.002
              if stall > 200:
                 q = 0.9
           if stall > 300:
              sigma = min(1.0, 0.1 + 0.02*(stall-300)/100)
              cur = perturb_log(best_a, rng, sigma)
              cur_score = fast_score(cur,Lc)
              lm=1.0; stall=0

    # final from best maybe polish? return

Euclidean run: Could be less effective but include if time. Let's implement a simpler projected subgradient with line search and restarts, using full weights/FFT gradient maybe.
```
def project_simplex(v):
   n=len(v)
   s=v.sum()
   # standard assuming target sum 1
   u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
   cssv=np.cumsum(u)
   cond=u*np.arange(1,n+1) > cssv - 1.0
   if not cond.any(): return np.full(n,1/n)
   rho=np.where(cond)[0][-1]
   theta=(cssv[rho]-1.0)/(rho+1.0)
   return np.maximum(v-theta,0.0)
```
If v sum not 1, projection to sum1 as above.

def run_projected(a,n,deadline,rng,tag):
    x=normalize_arr(a); x_prev=x.copy(); t=1.0; eta=1.0; best=...
    it=0; stall=0
    while time:
       t_old=t; t=0.5*(1+sqrt(1+4*t_old*t_old)); mom=(t_old-1)/t
       y=x + mom*(x-x_prev)
       y=np.maximum(y,0); sy=y.sum(); if sy<1e-15: y=x.copy() else y/=sy
       c=conv_square_fft(y,Lc); cur=2nM
       if cur<best: update
       w=weights_from_c(c,qproj)
       g=grad_weighted_fft(y,w,Lg,2.0)
       # line search eta candidates; maybe scale eta=2n? For Euclidean, gradient components ~1/n, step eta=1 may move 1/n. Use candidates [eta, eta*0.5, eta*2, eta*4]
       best_xn=None; best_sc=cur
       for et in [eta, eta*0.5, eta*2.0, eta*4.0]:
           xn=project_simplex(y - et*g)
           sc=fast_score(xn,Lc)
           if sc < best_sc:
               best_sc=sc; best_xn=xn; best_et=et
       if best_xn is not None:
           x_prev=x; x=best_xn; eta=min(best_et*1.1, 100.0); stall=0
       else:
           x_prev=x; eta*=0.7; stall+=1
           if stall>200: x=perturb(best_a); y=x? eta=1; stall=0; t=1
       it++
```

Let's write `run_mirror` more concretely:
```
def run_mirror(a, n, deadline, rng, tag):
    Lc = get_fft_len(2*n-1)
    Lg = get_fft_len(3*n-2)
    cur = normalize_arr(a)
    best_a = cur.copy()
    best_score = fast_score(cur, Lc)
    cur_score = best_score
    lm = 1.0
    q = 0.995
    stall = 0
    it = 0
    start = time.monotonic()
    last_out = start
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        c = conv_square_fft(cur, Lc)
        M = float(c.max())
        cur_score = 2.0*n*M
        if cur_score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = cur_score; best_a = cur.copy(); stall=0; print maybe
        if M <= 0: break
        if c.min() >= M*0.999999: # nearly flat
            # perturb best
            cur = perturb_log(best_a, rng, 0.05)
            cur_score = fast_score(cur,Lc)
            lm=1.0; stall=0; it+=1; continue
        w = weights_from_c(c, q, 1e-4)
        g = grad_weighted_fft(cur, w, Lg, 2.0)
        p1 = mirror_step(cur, g, lm)
        sc1 = fast_score(p1, Lc)
        chosen = None; chosen_score = cur_score; chosen_lm = lm
        # primary try current lm
        if sc1 < cur_score - 1e-14:
            chosen = p1; chosen_score = sc1; chosen_lm = lm
        # if improves, maybe try double to see further (only sometimes)
        if chosen is not None and (it % 5 == 0 or sc1 < best_score - 1e-9):
            p2 = mirror_step(cur, g, lm*2.0)
            sc2 = fast_score(p2, Lc)
            if sc2 < chosen_score - 1e-14:
                chosen = p2; chosen_score = sc2; chosen_lm = lm*2.0
        if chosen is None:
            # try half
            p3 = mirror_step(cur, g, lm*0.5)
            sc3 = fast_score(p3, Lc)
            if sc3 < cur_score - 1e-14:
                chosen = p3; chosen_score = sc3; chosen_lm = lm*0.5
        if chosen is not None:
            cur = chosen; cur_score = chosen_score; lm = chosen_lm
            if lm > 50: lm=50
            if lm < 0.02: lm=0.02
            # adjust: if improvement, slightly increase; else keep/reduce
            if cur_score >= best_score - 1e-12:
                lm *= 1.15
            else:
                lm *= 0.9
            if lm > 50: lm=50
            stall = 0
        else:
            # no descent; occasional nonmonotone
            if stall > 30 and rng.random() < 0.1:
                if sc1 < best_score * 1.02 and sc1 < cur_score * 1.2:
                    cur = p1; cur_score = sc1; lm = lm*0.8; stall = max(0, stall//2); continue
            lm *= 0.7
            stall += 1
            if stall > 80:
                if q < 0.999:
                    q = min(0.999, q + 0.004)
                else:
                    q = 0.90
                    stall = 10
            if stall > 300:
                sigma = 0.08 + 0.02*(stall-300)/100
                cur = perturb_log(best_a, rng, min(1.0, sigma))
                cur_score = fast_score(cur, Lc)
                if cur_score < best_score:
                    best_score = cur_score; best_a=cur.copy()
                lm = 1.0; stall=0; q=0.995
        it += 1
        if time.monotonic() - last_out > 5.0:
            print(f"[{tag}] it={it} best={best_score:.8f} cur={cur_score:.8f} lm={lm:.2f} q={q:.3f}")
            last_out = time.monotonic()
    # final score from best
    return best_a, best_score
```
This runs until deadline. It may call many `fast_score` (up to 3 per iter). Time maybe okay. For n=4806, FFT length 16384; 3 conv per iter maybe 3*~0.1ms=0.3ms plus gradient FFT 0.1 -> 0.4ms? Python overhead maybe 1ms; 10000 iter 10s? Actually FFT 16k in numpy ~0.05ms? Could be 0.3ms; total 1-2s for 10000. Fine.

But `weights_from_c` uses `np.quantile` each iter O(m logm) maybe 9611, okay but maybe 0.1ms. `argpartition` avoided. Good.

Potential issue: `np.quantile` with float q close to 1 (0.999) for 9611 returns near max; tau small. If c has many near equal, floor. Good.

`perturb_log`:
```
def perturb_log(a, rng, sigma):
    floor=1e-18
    p=np.maximum(a, floor)
    lp=np.log(p)
    noise=rng.normal(0.0, sigma, p.size)
    lp=lp+noise
    lp-=np.max(lp)
    q=np.exp(lp)
    return normalize_arr(q)
```
If sigma large, some lp very negative; exp maybe underflow to 0, okay normalized. If all underflow? max subtract ensures at least one exp(0)=1.

`normalize_arr` if q all zeros? It handles.

`run_projected` details:
```
def run_projected(a, n, deadline, rng, tag):
   Lc=...; Lg=...
   x=normalize_arr(a); x_prev=x.copy(); t=1.0; eta=1.0; best...
   q=0.995; it=0; stall=0
   while time < deadline:
      t_old=t; t=0.5*(1+sqrt(1+4*t_old*t_old)); mom=(t_old-1)/t
      y=x + mom*(x-x_prev)
      y=np.maximum(y,0); sy=y.sum(); if sy<1e-15: y=x.copy(); else: y/=sy
      c=conv_square_fft(y,Lc); cur=2nM
      if cur<best update
      w=weights_from_c(c,q,1e-4)
      g=grad_weighted_fft(y,w,Lg,2.0)
      best_xn=None; best_sc=cur; best_et=eta
      # candidate etas depending on eta
      for factor in (1.0, 0.5, 2.0, 0.25, 4.0):
          et=eta*factor
          if et <=0 or not finite: continue
          xn=project_simplex(y - et*g)
          sc=fast_score(xn,Lc)
          if sc < best_sc - 1e-14:
             best_sc=sc; best_xn=xn; best_et=et
      if best_xn is not None:
          x_prev=x; x=best_xn; eta=min(max(best_et*1.05,0.1),1000.0); stall=0
      else:
          x_prev=x; eta=eta*0.7
          stall+=1
          if stall>200: x=perturb_log(best_a,rng,0.1); eta=1.0; t=1.0; stall=0
      if time - last_print >5: print
   return best
```
Step eta as Euclidean, not standardized. If gradient norm small, eta unchanged. Line search adapts. `project_simplex` of y - et*g; if et large and g has large variance, projection picks support. Good.
- If y already best and no descent, we keep x=y (no move). Actually `x_prev=x` and x unchanged? If best_xn None, we should set `x = y.copy()`? In FISTA, if no line search success, often set x_new = y (or previous). We can set x = y.copy() to ensure progress? But y differs from x due to momentum; moving to y without gradient could be arbitrary. Maybe set x = x.copy() and reduce eta. x_prev? Need avoid division by zero. If no move, set `x_prev = x.copy()` (so momentum zero next) and `x` unchanged. But standard a_prev=x_prev? We'll do:
```
if accept: x_prev = x.copy(); x=best_xn; t=1? no
else: x_prev = x.copy(); eta*=0.7
```
Then next mom = (t_old-1)/t with x-x_prev=0 => y=x. Good.
- If accept after line search, set t maybe reset if stalling? Keep.
- `q` adapt if stall.

def next_pow2(x):

Detailed helper implementation:
```
import numpy as np
import time
from operator import itemgetter

def next_pow2(x): ...
def normalize_arr(a): ...
def conv_square_fft(a, L): ... # require L not None? We'll pass L.

def weights_from_c(c, q, rel_floor):
    m=c.size
    maxc=float(c.max())
    if maxc <= 1e-300:
       w=np.full(m,1.0/m)
       return w
    if q >= 1.0:
       idx=int(m-1)
       # top 10? Let's if q==1 use top 32 uniform to avoid all max? But we don't pass q=1.
    thresh=float(np.quantile(c, q))
    tau=max(maxc-thresh, maxc*rel_floor, 1e-300)
    z=(c-maxc)/tau
    z=np.clip(z, -50.0, 0.0)
    w=np.exp(z)
    s=w.sum()
    if s <= 0: w.fill(1.0/m)
    else: w/=s
    # maybe add tiny noise outside handle
    return w
```
If q very close to 1, `np.quantile(c, q)` with q=0.9999 for m=9611 returns close max. ok. If q<=0, error; ensure q in [0,1].

`grad_weighted_fft(a, w, L):`
```
def grad_weighted_fft(a, w, L, factor=2.0):
    n=len(a)
    r=a[::-1]
    Rp=np.fft.rfft(r, L)
    Wp=np.fft.rfft(w, L)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(Rp*Wp, L)
    g=conv[n-1:2*n-1].copy()
    g *= factor
    return g
```
Potential if L < 3n-2? ensure.

`mirror_step` as above.

`perturb_log`:
```
def perturb_log(a, rng, sigma):
    p=np.maximum(a, 1e-20)
    lp=np.log(p)
    noise=rng.normal(0.0, sigma, p.size)
    lp=lp+noise
    lp-=np.max(lp)
    q=np.exp(lp)
    return normalize_arr(q)
```

`project_simplex`:
```
def project_simplex(v):
    n=v.size
    if n==0: return v
    u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv=np.cumsum(u)
    idx=np.arange(1,n+1,dtype=np.float64)
    cond=u*idx > (cssv - 1.0)
    if not np.any(cond):
       return np.full(n, 1.0/n)
    rho=np.where(cond)[0][-1]
    theta=(cssv[rho]-1.0)/(rho+1.0)
    return np.maximum(v-theta,0.0)
```
This projects to sum=1. If v all nan? We clean before. Good.

def power_vector(n, beta):

   d[0]=1.0
   factor=1.0

```
"""..."""
import numpy as np
import time
from operator import itemgetter

def next_pow2(x):
    x=int(x)
    if x <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (x - 1).bit_length()

def normalize_arr(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1,dtype=np.float64)
    bad = ~np.isfinite(a)
    if np.any(bad):
        a = a.copy()
        a[bad] = 0.0
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(a.shape, 1.0/float(a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    a = a / s
    return a
```
If a shape 2d? We only use 1d. fine.

`conv_square_fft`:
```
def conv_square_fft(a, L):
    n=len(a)
    out=2*n-1
    A=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A, L)
    c = c[:out]
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c
```
Need `L` integer.

`weights_from_c`:
```
def weights_from_c(c, q, rel_floor):
    m=c.size
    if m <=0: return np.empty(0)
    maxc=float(c.max())
    if maxc <= 1e-300:
       return np.full(m,1.0/float(m))
    if q <=0: q=0.0
    elif q >=1.0: q=0.999999
    thresh=float(np.quantile(c,q))
    tau=max(maxc-thresh, maxc*rel_floor, 1e-300)
    z=(c-maxc)/tau
    z=np.clip(z, -50.0, 0.0)
    w=np.exp(z)
    sw=float(w.sum())
    if sw <=1e-300:
       w.fill(1.0/float(m))
    else:
       w /= sw
    return w
```
If c has many exact max, quantile maybe max, tau floor, all max get weight1, lower maybe exp(-large) zero if differences >50 tau. Good.

`grad_weighted_fft`:
```
def grad_weighted_fft(a,w,L,factor=2.0):
   n=len(a)
   if n == 0: return np.empty(0)
   r=a[::-1]
   Rp=np.fft.rfft(r, L)
   Wp=np.fft.rfft(w, L)
   conv=np.fft.irfft(Rp*Wp, L)
   g=conv[n-1:2*n-1].copy()
   g *= factor
   return g
```
If `w` length 2n-1, L≥3n-2. Good.

`mirror_step`:
```
def mirror_step(a,g,lm,floor=1e-18):
    g = np.asarray(g, dtype=np.float64)
    g = g - float(np.mean(g))
    denom = float(np.sqrt(np.mean(g*g)))
    if denom <= 1e-30:
       return a.copy()
    alpha = -float(lm) * g / denom
    alpha = np.clip(alpha, -30.0, 30.0)
    a2 = a * np.exp(alpha)
    a2 = np.where(a2 > floor, a2, floor)
    s = float(a2.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
       return a.copy()
    a2 = a2 / s
    return a2
```
`np.where` may create array; okay.

`perturb_log`:
```
def perturb_log(a, rng, sigma):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    p=np.maximum(a,1e-20)
    lp=np.log(p)
    noise=rng.normal(0.0, float(sigma), p.size)
    lp=lp+noise
    lp-=float(np.max(lp))
    q=np.exp(lp)
    return normalize_arr(q)
```

   Lc,Lg

      c=conv_square_fft(y,Lc); s=float(y.sum()); cur=2nM/(s*s)

"""
Search for a nonnegative sequence minimizing max(np.convolve(a,a)) * 2n / sum(a)^2.
Uses multiplicative (mirror) descent with softmax objective, FFT convolution,
FFT-weighted gradients, multiple restarts, and a projected-gradient polish.
"""

import time
from operator import itemgetter
...

Let's code `run_mirror` fully:

```
def run_mirror(a, n, deadline, rng, tag, outside_best_a=None, outside_best_score=None):
    Lc = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    Lg = next_pow2(3 * n - 2)
    cur = normalize_arr(a)
    best_a = cur.copy()
    best_score = fast_score(cur, Lc)
    if outside_best_a is not None and outside_best_score is not None:
        if float(outside_best_score) < best_score:
            best_a = np.asarray(outside_best_a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
            if best_a.size != n:
                # resize? should not
                best_a = normalize_arr(best_a)
            best_score = float(outside_best_score)
    cur_score = fast_score(cur, Lc)
    if cur_score < best_score:
        best_a = cur.copy(); best_score = cur_score
    lm = 1.0
    q = 0.98
    stall = 0
    it = 0
    last_print = time.monotonic()
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        c = conv_square_fft(cur, Lc)
        M = float(c.max())
        s = float(cur.sum())
        if s > 1e-300:
            cur_score = float(2.0 * n * M / (s * s))
        else:
            cur_score = np.inf
        if cur_score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = cur_score
            best_a = cur.copy()
        spread = M - float(c.min())
        if spread <= max(1e-12, M * 1e-10):
            cur = perturb_log(best_a, rng, 0.05)
            cur_score = fast_score(cur, Lc)
            if cur_score < best_score:
                best_score = cur_score; best_a = cur.copy()
            it += 1
            continue
        w = weights_from_c(c, q, 1e-4)
        if stall > 25:
            w = w + rng.random(c.size) * 1e-5
            wsum = float(w.sum())
            if wsum > 0:
                w /= wsum
        g = grad_weighted_fft(cur, w, Lg, 2.0)
        prev_score = cur_score
        p1 = mirror_step(cur, g, lm)
        sc1 = fast_score(p1, Lc)
        chosen = None
        chosen_score = prev_score
        chosen_lm = lm
        if sc1 < prev_score - 1e-13:
            chosen = p1
            chosen_score = sc1
            chosen_lm = lm
            if (it % 5 == 0) or (sc1 < best_score - 1e-8):
                p2 = mirror_step(cur, g, lm * 2.0)
                sc2 = fast_score(p2, Lc)
                if sc2 < chosen_score - 1e-13:
                    chosen = p2
                    chosen_score = sc2
                    chosen_lm = lm * 2.0
        if chosen is None:
            p3 = mirror_step(cur, g, lm * 0.5)
            sc3 = fast_score(p3, Lc)
            if sc3 < prev_score - 1e-13:
                chosen = p3
                chosen_score = sc3
                chosen_lm = lm * 0.5
        if chosen is not None:
            cur = chosen
            cur_score = chosen_score
            if cur_score < best_score:
                best_score = cur_score
                best_a = cur.copy()
            lm *= 1.05
            if lm > 50.0:
                lm = 50.0
            if lm < 0.02:
                lm = 0.02
            stall = 0
        else:
            if stall > 40 and rng.random() < 0.08:
                if sc1 < best_score * 1.02 and sc1 < prev_score * 1.25:
                    cur = p1
                    cur_score = sc1
                    lm *= 0.8
                    stall = max(0, stall // 2)
                    it += 1
                    continue
            lm *= 0.7
            if lm < 0.02:
                lm = 0.02
            stall += 1
            if stall > 70:
                if q < 0.999:
                    q = min(0.999, q + 0.005)
                else:
                    q = 0.90
                    stall = 12
            if stall > 200:
                sigma = 0.08 + 0.02 * min(10.0, float(stall - 200) / 50.0)
                cur = perturb_log(best_a, rng, sigma)
                cur_score = fast_score(cur, Lc)
                if cur_score < best_score:
                    best_score = cur_score
                    best_a = cur.copy()
                lm = 1.0
                q = 0.98
                stall = 0
        it += 1
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now - last_print > 5.0:
            print(f"[{tag}] it={it} best={best_score:.8f} cur={cur_score:.8f} lm={lm:.3f} q={q:.3f}")
            last_print = now
    final_score = fast_score(best_a, Lc)
    return best_a, final_score
```
Potential issue: `cur_score` might be `np.inf` if cur sum zero? normalize ensures sum1. okay.

`fast_score` returns inf if s<1e-300. Good.

In `run_projected`, use similar outside best.

```
def run_projected(...):
   ...
   cur_score=fast_score(x,Lc)
   if cur_score<best_score...
   while:
       ... y normalized
       c=conv_square_fft(y,Lc); s=float(y.sum()); cur=...
       if cur<best update
       if c.max() <=0? no
       w=weights_from_c(c,0.98,1e-4)
       g=grad_weighted_fft(y,w,Lg,2.0)
       best_xn=None; best_sc=cur; best_et=eta
       for fac in (...):
           et = eta*fac
           if not np.isfinite(et) or et <=0: continue
           xn=project_simplex(y - et*g)
           sc=fast_score(xn,Lc)
           if sc < best_sc - 1e-13:
               best_sc=sc; best_xn=xn
       if best_xn is not None:
           x_prev=x.copy(); x=best_xn; eta=best_et*1.05; eta=clip; stall=0
       else:
           x_prev=x.copy(); eta*=0.7; stall +=1
           if stall > 200:
              x=perturb_log(best_a,rng,0.1); x_prev=x.copy(); eta=1.0; t=1.0; stall=0
       it++ print
   final fast_score best
```

```python
"""
Search for a nonnegative sequence minimizing
    2*n*max(convolve(a,a)) / sum(a)^2.

The code uses:
- FFT-based convolution for fast objective evaluation.
- Mirrordescent / multiplicative projected gradient updates in log-space.
- Softmax weights over convolution coefficients to smooth the max objective.
- FFT-based weighted gradient computation.
- Multiple candidate starts (previous construction, power laws, tapered
  square-root shapes, random perturbations).
- Adaptive step sizes, quantile focus, nonmonotone accepts, and restarts.
- A projected-gradient/FISTA polish phase.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
from operator import itemgetter


def next_pow2(x):
    """Smallest power of two >= x."""
    x = int(x)
    if x <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (x - 1).bit_length()


def normalize_arr(a):
    """Clip to nonnegative finite floats and normalize to unit sum."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)

    bad = ~np.isfinite(a)
    if np.any(bad):
        a = a.copy()
        a[bad] = 0.0

    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(a.shape, 1.0 / float(a.size), dtype=np.float64)

    return a / s


def conv_square_fft(a, L):
    """Full linear convolution a*a via FFT, assuming L is large enough."""
    n = len(a)
    out_len = 2 * n - 1
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, L)
    c = c[:out_len]
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c


def fast_score(a, L):
    """Fast evaluation of the objective for any scaling of a."""
    n = len(a)
    c = conv_square_fft(a, L)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 1e-300:
        return np.inf
    M = float(c.max())
    return float(2.0 * n * M / (s * s))


def weights_from_c(c, q, rel_floor):
    """
    Softmax weights on convolution coefficients.

    q controls the quantile used for the temperature.  Smaller q gives
    broader weights; q close to 1 focuses on the largest coefficients.
    """
    m = c.size
    if m <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)

    maxc = float(c.max())
    if maxc <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(m, 1.0 / float(m), dtype=np.float64)

    if q <= 0.0:
        q = 0.0
    elif q >= 1.0:
        q = 0.999999

    thresh = float(np.quantile(c, q))
    tau = max(maxc - thresh, maxc * float(rel_floor), 1e-300)

    z = (c - maxc) / tau
    z = np.clip(z, -50.0, 0.0)
    w = np.exp(z)

    sw = float(w.sum())
    if sw <= 1e-300:
        w = np.full(m, 1.0 / float(m), dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        w /= sw

    return w


def grad_weighted_fft(a, w, L, factor=2.0):
    """
    Gradient of sum_k w_k * c_k(c = a*a).

    For self-convolution, factor=2.0.  Uses an FFT with a reversed array:
        grad_i = factor * sum_j w_{i+j} a_j.
    """
    n = len(a)
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)

    r = a[::-1]
    Rp = np.fft.rfft(r, L)
    Wp = np.fft.rfft(w, L)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(Rp * Wp, L)

    g = conv[n - 1:2 * n - 1].copy()
    g *= float(factor)
    return g


def mirror_step(a, g, lm, floor=1e-18):
    """
    Multiplicative/mirror projected gradient step.

    lm is a standardized step size: exp(-lm * standardized_gradient).
    """
    g = np.asarray(g, dtype=np.float64)
    g = g - float(np.mean(g))
    denom = float(np.sqrt(np.mean(g * g)))
    if denom <= 1e-30:
        return a.copy()

    alpha = -float(lm) * g / denom
    alpha = np.clip(alpha, -30.0, 30.0)

    a2 = a * np.exp(alpha)
    a2 = np.where(a2 > floor, a2, floor)
    s = float(a2.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return a.copy()

    return a2 / s


def perturb_log(a, rng, sigma):
    """Perturb in log-space and renormalize."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.maximum(a, 1e-20)
    lp = np.log(p)
    noise = rng.normal(0.0, float(sigma), p.size)
    lp = lp + noise
    lp -= float(np.max(lp))
    q = np.exp(lp)
    return normalize_arr(q)


def project_simplex(v):
    """Euclidean projection onto the probability simplex sum(a)=1, a>=0."""
    n = v.size
    if n == 0:
        return v.copy()

    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv = np.cumsum(u)
    idx = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    cond = u * idx > (cssv - 1.0)

    if not np.any(cond):
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=np.float64)

    rho = np.where(cond)[0][-1]
    theta = (cssv[rho] - 1.0) / float(rho + 1)
    return np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)


def power_vector(n, beta):
    """Unnormalized power-law vector (i+1)^(-beta)."""
    x = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    return np.power(x, -float(beta))


def sqrt_vector(n):
    """
    Coefficients of (1-z)^(-1/2), truncated to degree n-1.

    This is a strong nonuniform initializer because its self-convolution
    is exactly flat for the first n coefficients.
    """
    d = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    d[0] = 1.0
    for i in range(n - 1):
        d[i + 1] = d[i] * (2.0 * i + 1.0) / (2.0 * (i + 1.0))
    return d


def run_mirror(a, n, deadline, rng, tag, outside_best_a=None, outside_best_score=None):
    """
    Mirror-descent search using softmax-weighted gradients.
    """
    Lc = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    Lg = next_pow2(3 * n - 2)

    cur = normalize_arr(a)
    best_a = cur.copy()
    best_score = fast_score(cur, Lc)

    if outside_best_a is not None and outside_best_score is not None:
        obs = float(outside_best_score)
        if obs < best_score:
            best_a = np.asarray(outside_best_a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
            if best_a.size != n:
                best_a = normalize_arr(best_a)
            best_score = obs

    cur_score = fast_score(cur, Lc)
    if cur_score < best_score:
        best_a = cur.copy()
        best_score = float(cur_score)

    lm = 1.0
    q = 0.98
    stall = 0
    it = 0
    last_print = time.monotonic()

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        c = conv_square_fft(cur, Lc)
        M = float(c.max())
        s = float(cur.sum())
        if s > 1e-300:
            cur_score = float(2.0 * n * M / (s * s))
        else:
            cur_score = np.inf

        if cur_score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = float(cur_score)
            best_a = cur.copy()

        spread = M - float(c.min())
        if spread <= max(1e-12, M * 1e-10):
            cur = perturb_log(best_a, rng, 0.05)
            cur_score = fast_score(cur, Lc)
            if cur_score < best_score:
                best_score = float(cur_score)
                best_a = cur.copy()
            it += 1
            continue

        w = weights_from_c(c, q, 1e-4)

        # Small stochastic tie-breaker when stuck.
        if stall > 25:
            w = w + rng.random(c.size) * 1e-5
            ws = float(w.sum())
            if ws > 0.0:
                w /= ws

        g = grad_weighted_fft(cur, w, Lg, 2.0)

        prev_score = cur_score
        p1 = mirror_step(cur, g, lm)
        sc1 = fast_score(p1, Lc)

        chosen = None
        chosen_score = prev_score
        chosen_lm = lm

        if sc1 < prev_score - 1e-13:
            chosen = p1
            chosen_score = sc1
            chosen_lm = lm

            # Occasionally probe a larger step when things are improving.
            if (it % 5 == 0) or (sc1 < best_score - 1e-8):
                p2 = mirror_step(cur, g, lm * 2.0)
                sc2 = fast_score(p2, Lc)
                if sc2 < chosen_score - 1e-13:
                    chosen = p2
                    chosen_score = sc2
                    chosen_lm = lm * 2.0

        if chosen is None:
            p3 = mirror_step(cur, g, lm * 0.5)
            sc3 = fast_score(p3, Lc)
            if sc3 < prev_score - 1e-13:
                chosen = p3
                chosen_score = sc3
                chosen_lm = lm * 0.5

        if chosen is not None:
            cur = chosen
            cur_score = chosen_score
            if cur_score < best_score:
                best_score = float(cur_score)
                best_a = cur.copy()

            lm *= 1.05
            if lm > 50.0:
                lm = 50.0
            if lm < 0.02:
                lm = 0.02
            stall = 0
        else:
            # Occasional controlled nonmonotone move.
            if stall > 40 and rng.random() < 0.08:
                if sc1 < best_score * 1.02 and sc1 < prev_score * 1.25:
                    cur = p1
                    cur_score = sc1
                    lm *= 0.8
                    stall = max(0, stall // 2)
                    it += 1
                    continue

            lm *= 0.7
            if lm < 0.02:
                lm = 0.02
            stall += 1

            if stall > 70:
                if q < 0.999:
                    q = min(0.999, q + 0.005)
                else:
                    q = 0.90
                    stall = 12

            if stall > 200:
                sigma = 0.08 + 0.02 * min(10.0, float(stall - 200) / 50.0)
                cur = perturb_log(best_a, rng, sigma)
                cur_score = fast_score(cur, Lc)
                if cur_score < best_score:
                    best_score = float(cur_score)
                    best_a = cur.copy()
                lm = 1.0
                q = 0.98
                stall = 0

        it += 1
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now - last_print > 5.0:
            print(f"[{tag}] it={it} best={best_score:.8f} cur={cur_score:.8f} lm={lm:.3f} q={q:.3f}")
            last_print = now

    final_score = fast_score(best_a, Lc)
    return best_a, float(final_score)


def run_projected(a, n, deadline, rng, tag, outside_best_a=None, outside_best_score=None):
    """
    Projected gradient / FISTA-style polish using the same weighted gradients.
    """
    Lc = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    Lg = next_pow2(3 * n - 2)

    x = normalize_arr(a)
    x_prev = x.copy()
    t = 1.0
    eta = 1.0

    best_a = x.copy()
    best_score = fast_score(x, Lc)

    if outside_best_a is not None and outside_best_score is not None:
        obs = float(outside_best_score)
        if obs < best_score:
            best_a = np.asarray(outside_best_a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
            if best_a.size != n:
                best_a = normalize_arr(best_a)
            best_score = obs

    cur_score = fast_score(x, Lc)
    if cur_score < best_score:
        best_a = x.copy()
        best_score = float(cur_score)

    stall = 0
    it = 0
    last_print = time.monotonic()

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        t_old = t
        t = 0.5 * (1.0 + np.sqrt(1.0 + 4.0 * t_old * t_old))
        if t > 10000.0:
            t = 1.0
            x_prev = x.copy()

        mom = (t_old - 1.0) / t
        y = x + mom * (x - x_prev)
        y = np.maximum(y, 0.0)
        sy = float(y.sum())
        if sy < 1e-300:
            y = x.copy()
        else:
            y /= sy

        c = conv_square_fft(y, Lc)
        M = float(c.max())
        s = float(y.sum())
        if s > 1e-300:
            cur = float(2.0 * n * M / (s * s))
        else:
            cur = np.inf

        if cur < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = float(cur)
            best_a = y.copy()

        w = weights_from_c(c, 0.98, 1e-4)
        g = grad_weighted_fft(y, w, Lg, 2.0)

        best_xn = None
        best_sc = cur
        best_et = eta

        for fac in (1.0, 0.5, 2.0, 0.25, 4.0):
            et = eta * fac
            if not np.isfinite(et) or et <= 0.0:
                continue
            xn = project_simplex(y - et * g)
            sc = fast_score(xn, Lc)
            if sc < best_sc - 1e-13:
                best_sc = sc
                best_xn = xn
                best_et = et

        if best_xn is not None:
            x_prev = x.copy()
            x = best_xn
            eta = best_et * 1.05
            if eta > 1000.0:
                eta = 1000.0
            if eta < 0.1:
                eta = 0.1
            stall = 0
        else:
            x_prev = x.copy()
            eta *= 0.7
            stall += 1

            if stall > 200:
                x = perturb_log(best_a, rng, 0.1)
                x_prev = x.copy()
                eta = 1.0
                t = 1.0
                stall = 0

        it += 1
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now - last_print > 5.0:
            print(f"[{tag}] it={it} best={best_score:.8f} cur={cur:.8f} eta={eta:.3f}")
            last_print = now

    final_score = fast_score(best_a, Lc)
    return best_a, float(final_score)


def propose_candidate(budget_s=2400):
    """
    Main search entry point.
    """
    try:
        budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget = 2400.0

    start_time = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start_time + max(60.0, budget - 45.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(20240524)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Determine length and initial given construction.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    arr = None
    n = 0
    try:
        arr = np.array(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
        n = int(arr.size)
    except Exception:
        arr = None
        n = 4806

    if n < 32:
        n = 4806
        arr = None

    if arr is None:
        if n <= 0:
            n = 4806
        arr = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        arr = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, None)
        arr[~np.isfinite(arr)] = 0.0
        n = int(arr.size)
        if np.sum(arr) <= 1e-300:
            arr = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)

    if n > 10000:
        target = 8000
        block = max(1, n // target)
        if block > 1:
            use = block * target
            arr = arr[:use].reshape(target, block).mean(axis=1)
        else:
            arr = arr[:target]
        n = int(arr.size)

    Lc = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Candidate initializers.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    candidates = []

    if arr is not None:
        candidates.append(arr)

    candidates.append(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))

    for beta in (0.25, 0.40, 0.50, 0.60, 0.70, 0.85):
        candidates.append(power_vector(n, beta))

    sv = sqrt_vector(n)
    candidates.append(sv)

    xgrid = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)

    for gamma in (0.2, 0.5, 1.0, 1.5, -0.3):
        candidates.append(sv * (1.0 + gamma * xgrid))

    for gammal in (0.5, 1.0, 2.0):
        candidates.append(sv * np.exp(gammal * xgrid))

    candidates.append(rng.exponential(1.0, n))

    base_lp = np.log(np.maximum(sv, 1e-15))
    for sig in (0.05, 0.10, 0.20):
        lp = base_lp + rng.normal(0.0, sig, n)
        candidates.append(np.exp(lp - lp.max()))

    for _ in range(8):
        beta = 0.3 + 0.7 * rng.random()
        gammal = rng.uniform(-0.5, 2.0)
        p = power_vector(n, beta) * np.exp(gammal * xgrid)
        candidates.append(p)

    if arr is not None and float(np.sum(arr)) > 1e-300:
        candidates.append(0.7 * arr + 0.3 * sv)
        candidates.append(0.5 * arr + 0.5 * sv)
        candidates.append(0.3 * arr + 0.7 * sv)
        candidates.append(0.5 * arr + 0.5 * np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Score candidates and sort.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    scored = []
    for cand in candidates:
        p = normalize_arr(cand)
        sc = fast_score(p, Lc)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            scored.append((float(sc), p))

    if not scored:
        p = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        scored = [(2.0, p)]

    scored.sort(key=itemgetter(0))

    global_best_a = scored[0][1].copy()
    global_best_score = float(scored[0][0])

    print(f"Initial length: {n}")
    print(f"Initial best score: {global_best_score:.8f}")

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Main mirror-descent phases.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    def remaining_time():
        return max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic())

    # Primary long run from best initializer.
    if remaining_time() > 120:
        alloc = min(900.0, 0.45 * remaining_time())
        if alloc > 60:
            end = time.monotonic() + alloc
            a_out, s_out = run_mirror(
                scored[0][1], n, end, rng, "mirror0",
                global_best_a, global_best_score
            )
            if s_out < global_best_score:
                global_best_a = a_out.copy()
                global_best_score = float(s_out)
                print(f"After mirror0: {global_best_score:.8f}")

    # Secondary run from a different promising initializer.
    if remaining_time() > 120 and len(scored) >= 2:
        sc2 = scored[1][0]
        if sc2 < global_best_score + 0.10:
            alloc = min(300.0, 0.15 * remaining_time())
            if alloc > 60:
                end = time.monotonic() + alloc
                a_out, s_out = run_mirror(
                    scored[1][1], n, end, rng, "mirror1",
                    global_best_a, global_best_score
                )
                if s_out < global_best_score:
                    global_best_a = a_out.copy()
                    global_best_score = float(s_out)
                    print(f"After mirror1: {global_best_score:.8f}")

    # Tertiary run from another initializer if available and still promising.
    if remaining_time() > 100 and len(scored) >= 3:
        sc3 = scored[2][0]
        if sc3 < global_best_score + 0.12:
            alloc = min(240.0, 0.12 * remaining_time())
            if alloc > 60:
                end = time.monotonic() + alloc
                a_out, s_out = run_mirror(
                    scored[2][1], n, end, rng, "mirror2",
                    global_best_a, global_best_score
                )
                if s_out < global_best_score:
                    global_best_a = a_out.copy()
                    global_best_score = float(s_out)
                    print(f"After mirror2: {global_best_score:.8f}")

    # Projected-gradient polish.
    if remaining_time() > 120:
        end = min(deadline - 90.0, time.monotonic() + min(360.0, 0.18 * remaining_time()))
        if end > time.monotonic() + 60:
            a_out, s_out = run_projected(
                global_best_a, n, end, rng, "projected",
                global_best_a, global_best_score
            )
            if s_out < global_best_score:
                global_best_a = a_out.copy()
                global_best_score = float(s_out)
                print(f"After projected: {global_best_score:.8f}")

    # Final mirror polish from the current best until a small reserve remains.
    if remaining_time() > 90:
        end = deadline - 25.0
        a_out, s_out = run_mirror(
            global_best_a, n, end, rng, "polish",
            global_best_a, global_best_score
        )
        if s_out < global_best_score:
            global_best_a = a_out.copy()
            global_best_score = float(s_out)
            print(f"After polish: {global_best_score:.8f}")

    # Try a symmetrized version; sometimes averaging with reversal helps.
    sym = normalize_arr(0.5 * global_best_a + 0.5 * global_best_a[::-1])
    sym_score = fast_score(sym, Lc)
    if sym_score < global_best_score:
        global_best_a = sym.copy()
        global_best_score = float(sym_score)
        print(f"After symmetrization: {global_best_score:.8f}")

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Final verification with the official evaluator when available.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    final_list = [float(v) for v in global_best_a]
    best_direct = float(global_best_score)

    ev = globals().get("evaluate_sequence", None)
    if ev is not None:
        try:
            sc = ev(final_list)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and float(sc) < best_direct:
                best_direct = float(sc)
        except Exception:
            pass

        # Check a few strong fast-score candidates against the real evaluator.
        for idx in range(min(3, len(scored))):
            cand = scored[idx][1]
            cand_list = [float(v) for v in cand]
            if cand_list == final_list:
                continue
            try:
                sc = ev(cand_list)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and float(sc) < best_direct:
                    best_direct = float(sc)
                    final_list = cand_list
            except Exception:
                pass

    if not np.isfinite(best_direct):
        final_list = [1.0 / float(n) for _ in range(n)]

    return final_list
```
